{"parentUuid":"b6e40d78-13f1-4495-890b-2f0bd642bbfe","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"mcp_instructions_delta","addedNames":["plugin:context7:context7","plugin:serena:serena"],"addedBlocks":["## plugin:context7:context7\nUse this server to fetch current documentation whenever the user asks about a library, framework, SDK, API, CLI tool, or cloud service -- even well-known ones like React, Next.js, Prisma, Express, Tailwind, Django, or Spring Boot. This includes API syntax, configuration, version migration, library-specific debugging, setup instructions, and CLI tool usage. Use even when you think you know the answer -- your training data may not reflect recent changes. Prefer this over web search for library docs.\n\nDo not use for: refactoring, writing scripts from scratch, debugging business logic, code review, or general programming concepts.","## plugin:serena:serena\nCRITICAL: Before starting to work on a coding task, call the `initial_instructions` tool to read the 'Serena Instructions Manual'."],"removedNames":[]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"8854443f-e32c-44af-b0bd-31953d037e03","timestamp":"2026-05-09T04:38:25.567Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"6de7193e-f2d3-4b9f-a967-6dbdad109e56","version":"2.1.126","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"8854443f-e32c-44af-b0bd-31953d037e03","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- ccs-delegation: Auto-activate CCS CLI delegation for deterministic tasks. Parses user input, auto-selects optimal profile (glm/kimi/custom) from ~/.ccs/config.json, enhances prompts with context, executes via `ccs {profile} -p \"task\"` or `ccs {profile}:continue`, and reports results. Triggers on \"use ccs [task]\" patterns, typo/test/refactor keywords. Excludes complex architecture, security-critical code, performance optimization, breaking changes.\n- attach: Port the Claude Code harness layer from this project to a new project directory. Use when bootstrapping a fresh Claude Code project on the same machine, when peers want to copy the harness, or when verifying layer separation via dry-run. Reads .claude/manifest.yaml; copies harness-tagged artifacts only; excludes stockforge biz + personal layers; generates skeleton CLAUDE.md + manifest at target.\n- crawler-reliability: Build reliable web crawlers for data gathering. Use when implementing scrapers for CafeF, NDH, VietnamBiz, YouTube (yt-dlp), Facebook fanpages, or any external data source. Covers Python/Playwright patterns, retry/backoff, selector robustness, rate limiting, monitoring.\n- ddd-tactical-patterns: Apply DDD tactical patterns (aggregate, entity, value object, repository, domain event, anti-corruption layer) correctly in Python/dataclasses code. Use when designing or implementing domain models, repositories, or cross-bounded-context communication in StockForge's 9 BCs.\n- decompose-work: Split a non-trivial task into deterministic-vs-LLM portions before execution. Use when planning multi-step work where some sub-tasks are mechanical (file ops, regex, count, schema validation) and others require judgment (synthesis, design, ambiguity resolution). Outputs structured plan that maps each portion to the cheapest competent tool. Pairs with capability-map.md (model × effort × task_class grounding) and try-n-approaches (S9 — Karpathy autoresearch loop).\n- empirical-probe-first: Probe ALL viable strategies in a multi-option ladder BEFORE committing to one. Use when a session-plan / decision-ADR / spec presents ≥3 strategy options (e.g. \"Strategy A1/A2/A3\" or \"alternative B/C/D\") and the recommended option's source-evidence may be stale. Outputs a probe matrix per strategy + commit decision with empirical citation. Pairs with `vendor-api-probe.sh` (deterministic detection layer; SessionStart hook) and `decompose-work` (deterministic-vs-LLM split per probe). Charter promotion target — codifies L-S32-1 doctrine surfaced at S32 Track A pivot (master-plan recommended A2 vnstock alternate; empirical probe rejected A1+A2; A3 SSI direct httpx adopted instead).\n- evidence-extraction: Extract structured claims from unstructured sources with citation integrity. Use when building extraction pipelines, parsing news articles or KOL transcripts, or anywhere claims are created from raw data. Ensures every claim has source_url, extracted_at, confidence, and verified attribution. Enforces no-LLM-math invariant (I-S1).\n- fastapi-module: Implement FastAPI routers following StockForge conventions. Use when creating a new HTTP endpoint, adding routes, wiring dependency injection, configuring Pydantic DTOs. Covers router boundaries, provider patterns, testing setup. Phase 2+ only — Phase 1 uses Streamlit dashboard directly.\n- grill-maximization: Doctrine for bundling Q&A questions to the human at maximum density per turn (15-20 target, max 25), so each human-in-the-loop touchpoint is information-rich. Use when composing a Q&A bundle for human-workspace/q-and-a/pending/, or when deciding whether to ask one question or wait and bundle. Built on the principle that human-in-the-loop time is the scarcest resource in autonomous coding.\n- hook-diagnostics: Inspect hook event lifecycle (state machine + transcript cache + aggregator output) when triaging failed/stuck hooks or auditing self-awareness rollups. Use during sandwich-verifier review, drift investigation, or post-mortem when component-telemetry.jsonl + dispatch.jsonl + sessions-rollup.tsv need cross-checking.\n- obsidian-vault: Search, create, and manage notes in the StockForge Obsidian vault with wikilinks, frontmatter, and index maintenance. Use when adding content to the knowledge base, navigating entities/concepts, or maintaining the raw/wiki separation. Adapted from mattpocock/skills, MIT licensed. Extended with raw/wiki pattern from Karpathy LLM Wiki.\n- postgres-pgvector: Design Postgres schemas with pgvector and TimescaleDB for StockForge. Use when creating migrations, designing tables, writing queries. Covers vector embeddings for semantic search on Vietnamese text, time-series for price/sentiment data, JSONB vs structured columns.\n- promote-rule: Cluster agent-notes.md entries by lexical similarity, propose promotion of recurring rules to deterministic hook (priority 1), skill (priority 2), or charter (priority 3). Use when agent-notes has accumulated ≥10 new rules since last promotion run, or on-demand at phase boundary. Outputs proposal markdown to observations/. Pairs with capability-map.md (writes new task_class entries) and decompose-work (consumes promotion outcomes).\n- prompt-engineering: Design and optimize LLM prompts for StockForge pipeline stages. Use when writing or refining prompts for claim extraction, KOL recommendation extraction, sentiment analysis, thesis synthesis. Covers context assembly, prompt caching strategy, budget-aware prompts, structured output. Enforces no-LLM-math constraint.\n- qa-escalation: File-based Q&A protocol — composes bundle file in human-workspace/q-and-a/pending/, tracks lifecycle (pending → answered → stale → processed), creates URGENT notification when needed. Use when intent classifier recommends OPEN_QA_BUNDLE / ESCALATE_HUMAN, or grill-maximization has a bundle ready to write. Pairs with grill-maximization (composes content) + Track 5 hooks (auto-move stale).\n- session-memory-l0-l1: Extract structured memories (L0 quick metadata + L1 deep) from a Claude Code session JSONL transcript. L0 is pure regex (file paths, commands, errors, failures incl. Vietnamese phrases, next step). L1 ships the prompt builder + response parser + 15+35 head/tail windowing — caller dispatches the LLM (no auto-invoke in Phase 0). Plus cleanText harness-stripper + TranscriptCache (mtime+size dual-check + LRU). Use when ingesting a session for memory promotion, computing what files/commands/failures the session touched, or preparing an offline LLM extraction.\n- spec-dual-layer: Write specifications with dual-layer structure (business narrative + agent contract). Use when authoring new specs, refining existing specs, or understanding the spec template. Ensures both human stakeholder needs and agent execution needs are served.\n- spec-to-wiki: Convert raw specifications to Obsidian wiki format with wikilinks, frontmatter, and index updates. Use when adding a new spec to the knowledge base, or when regenerating wiki view after spec changes. Maintains raw as source of truth, wiki as navigable view.\n- sync-pull: On-demand confidence-score lookup before a SCOPE+/ARCH/IMPL decision. Reads agent-workspace/memory/sync-tracker/state.tsv + weights.yaml; surfaces the relevant category's score, tier, must-grill flag, and the decision-class threshold. Output drives \"self-decide vs grill\" choice. Use whenever an agent is about to commit a non-trivial decision and needs to ground confidence in historical hit rate per Charter Principle 8 (Calibration over confidence).\n- test-pyramid-balance: Balance unit, integration, and E2E tests properly in Python/pytest. Use when deciding what test to write, reviewing test coverage, or rebalancing skewed test suite. Covers test pyramid shape, BDD vs unit tests, when to use what. Includes stock-domain-specific test categories.\n- try-n-approaches: Karpathy autoresearch outer-loop framing — given a task or signal, generate ≥3 distinct approaches (DEEPEN / BROADEN / ABANDON families), pick one per cheap-first ordering, attach a deterministic metric function (per L-S12-1), and write the experiment frame to learning-data/loop/. Use when an open question surfaces from drift / dogfood / sync-grilling and you need to commit a measurable next step. Pairs with decompose-work (deterministic-vs-LLM split per approach) + capability-map (model × effort grounding) + promote-rule (consumes outcomes).\n- ubiquitous-language: Extract a DDD-style ubiquitous language glossary from the current conversation or domain area. Use when starting work on a new bounded context, when new domain terms emerge, or when verifying consistency between business language and code. Adapted from mattpocock/skills, MIT licensed.\n- user-prompt-intake: Hybrid intent classifier — main session lite-detects trivial prompts, dispatches intent-classifier subagent for complex ones. Use whenever a new user prompt lands (chat or file in human-workspace/user_prompt/) and you need to route the work. Built for full-autonomous loop where every prompt must be classified before action.\n- write-a-skill: Create new Claude Code skills with proper structure, progressive disclosure, and discoverability. Use when extracting a pattern from multiple sessions into a reusable skill, or when user asks to formalize a technique. {"parentUuid":"224b0be9-a82e-428e-b156-93d855b936b0","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"nested_memory","path":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\agent-workspace\\CLAUDE.md","content":{"path":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\agent-workspace\\CLAUDE.md","type":"Project","content":"# agent-workspace/ — Contract\n\n> **Audience**: agents working inside `agent-workspace/`.\n> **Established**: Decision 002 § Track 1 (Workspace Dualism Foundation).\n\n## Identity\n\n`agent-workspace/` is the **agent-owned execution + memory** layer. Everything here represents **agent state, decisions, plans, observations, learning**. Agents WRITE here freely (within constitution rules); humans READ here but normally do not edit.\n\nThis separation exists to prevent two failure modes from orch:\n1. **Charter drift via shared-workspace mutation** (orch CF-DOGFOOD-2)\n2. **Human cognitive overload from dense session logs** (UP02 §1.3 — \"human đối mặt hàng chục spec/document quá tải nếu không sắp xếp khoa học\")\n\nThe agent owns this directory but must keep it AUDITABLE — every artifact provenance-linked, every decision sourced.\n\n---\n\n## Subdirectories\n\n| Path | Purpose | Lifecycle |\n|---|---|---|\n| `constitution/` | **IMMUTABLE without explicit user approval**. Charter-tier rules (autonomous-protocol, model-routing, identity-scope, etc.). Track 7 produces drafts in `proposals/`; user explicit approve to move here. | Edit denied by .claude/settings.json; Write only via human approval gate |\n| `memory/project.md` | High-level project state; phase tracker; ADRs (last 5). Update on phase boundaries + architectural decisions. | Long-lived; trim to most recent decisions |\n| `memory/current-execution.md` | **THE single routing source of truth**. Active phase + active session + autonomous_mode flag + routing table. Read FIRST every session. | Long-lived; updated each session boundary |\n| `memory/agent-notes.md` | Learned rules from real experience (post-mortem / drift / user correction). Append-only mostly. | Long-lived |\n| `memory/decisions/` | Sequential ADRs (`NNN-<slug>.md`) + `_template.md` + `README.md` index. 12-field schema (Track 2). | Append-only; supersession via status field |\n| `memory/sessions/` | One file per session: `YYYY-MM-DD-session-N.md`. What happened, decisions made, files touched. | Append-only |\n| `memory/observations/` | Subagent return artifacts (per Track 6 spec). Each subagent writes one observation file describing what it did. | Append; cleaned periodically by aggregator |\n| `memory/checkpoints/` | Session handoff state for self-reboot (Track 5). `latest.md` is canonical pointer; timestamped historical files retained. | Append + latest.md updated each checkpoint |\n| `memory/patterns-discovered/` | Pattern mining outputs (Track 0 — DONE) + ongoing pattern catalog. | Append; significant patterns promoted to constitution |\n| `memory/drift-logs/` | Drift-check (DR1-DR12) results; runs on hook + on-demand. | Time-series append |\n| `memory/post-mortems/` | After significant failure or thesis-revoked event: what failed, root cause, prevention rule. | Append |\n| `memory/thesis-log/` | Stock-domain thesis exploration entries. Read-only for IMPL sessions. | Append + revisited per calibration |\n| `memory/sync-tracker/` | Track 8a output: Confidence Score SQLite + weights.yaml + auto-rendered `_index.md`. | Live updated by hooks |\n| `memory/self-awareness/` | Track 9 output: model × effort × thinking profile cards + known-issues + best-practices. | Live updated by Stop-hook aggregator |\n| `memory/mistake-log.md` | Track 7 deliverable: structured failure catalog. What went wrong / Root cause / Prevention rule / Severity. | Append; pre-flight read by all agents |\n| `session-plans/pending/` | Plans authored, awaiting execution. Phase 0 plan lives here. | Move to `completed/` when done |\n| `session-plans/completed/` | Executed plans; archived for reference + retrospective input. | Append |\n| `quality-reports/{deterministic,probabilistic,drift-reports}/` | Tier-1/2/3 gate outputs (CLAUDE.md § Quality Gates). | Per-run files |\n| `ubiquitous-language/` | DDD glossary + domain-mapping + drift-log. Stockforge stock-domain vocabulary. | Append; canonical via `/drill-me` |\n| `calibration/` | Empirical hit-rate data per signal type. Confidence claims MUST trace here (CLAUDE.md hard rule). | Append per thesis post-mortem |\n| `research/` | Research notes from `research-scanner` subagent dispatches (e.g., reference-repo studies). | Append |\n\n---\n\n## Contract Rules (BINDING)\n\n1. **Constitution is immutable absent explicit human approval.** `Edit/Write` to `agent-workspace/constitution/**` is in `.claude/settings.json` deny list. Track 7 writes constitution drafts to `proposals/`; user explicit approve moves them.\n\n2. **`memory/decisions/` is append-mostly + supersession-status.** Don't delete decisions; mark `status: superseded-by-D-NNN` to revoke. Sequential numbering (`001`, `002`, ...) — don't reuse numbers.\n\n3. **`current-execution.md` is the routing source-of-truth.** No skill, agent, or command may hardcode phase paths or session paths from memory; resolve via this file. Stale paths in CLAUDE.md or skills = anti-pattern.\n\n4. **Never edit `obsidian-vault/raw/`.** Immutable. Vault writes only to `wiki/`. (Established in stockforge CLAUDE.md.)\n\n5. **Provenance is mandatory.** Every decision file references its source (human prompt / spec clause / drift detection / agent inference). Every thesis cites source + as-of date (I-S2). Every confidence claim cites historical hit rate (I-S20-equivalent).\n\n6. **Agents never `git commit` in this directory unless user explicitly requests.** Stage only.\n\n7. **`patterns-discovered/` artifacts are READ-ONLY for production code.** Patterns must be promoted to constitution / skills / hook scripts before becoming binding. (Otherwise they're informal notes, not contracts.)\n\n---\n\n## Reading Priority for Agent\n\nPer `agent-workspace/constitution/autonomous-protocol.md` (Track 7) and stockforge `CLAUDE.md` § Session Protocol:\n\n1. **`memory/current-execution.md`** — first; resolve active track\n2. **`memory/project.md`** — project state\n3. **`memory/checkpoints/latest.md`** if a recent checkpoint exists (within last 24h) — resume context\n4. **`memory/agent-notes.md`** — learned rules\n5. **`memory/mistake-log.md`** — pre-flight failure catalog\n6. **Last 3 files in `memory/sessions/`** — recent context\n7. **`session-plans/pending/<active-plan>.md`** — current plan\n8. **Relevant constitution files** as task demands (don't load all every session)\n9. **Relevant skills + agents + commands** as task demands\n\n---\n\n## Anti-Patterns to Avoid\n\n(From `memory/patterns-discovered/SYNTHESIS.md` § 6, full catalog 23 items. Highest-priority for this directory:)\n\n- **AP-1 Same-agent self-review** — never review your own implementation; dispatch fresh-context verifier\n- **AP-2 Self-track wind-down** — `.transcript-tokens` real-transcript is authoritative, not LLM self-track\n- **AP-5 Charter-coherence defer overriding user-CRITICAL** — re-read all `user_prompt/*` at every phase entry; never silently defer USER-CRITICAL\n- **AP-7 Performative SC ticking** — defer with explicit prerequisites > fire vacuous proposals\n- **AP-8 Pre-staged work causing checkpoint drift** — update `current-execution.md` immediately on task complete, not session-end\n- **AP-17 Identity drift** — stockforge is AI-first VN stock advisory; not generic financial framework (see `constitution/identity-scope.md` once Track 7 ships)\n- **AP-23 Continuous LLM-Guardian** — deterministic hooks = Guardian; LLM Guardian only at session-end aggregation\n\n---\n\n## Connection to human-workspace/\n\nThis directory is the agent's domain. Human's domain is `human-workspace/`. The two communicate through:\n- Human → Agent: `human-workspace/user_prompt/`, `human-workspace/decisions/`\n- Agent → Human: `human-workspace/notifications/`, `human-workspace/q-and-a/pending/`\n- Bidirectional: `human-workspace/q-and-a/answered/` — agent writes pending; either human or agent (per Auto-mv rule below) moves resolved bundles to answered/\n- Audit trail: this directory's `memory/decisions/` references `human-workspace/` source files via path pointers\n\n**Auto-mv rule (HH-E.2 — D-031 ratification, 2026-05-05)**: Agent MAY mv a bundle from `q-and-a/pending/` to `q-and-a/answered/` IFF ALL of the following hold:\n\n1. **Frontmatter signal**: bundle frontmatter `status:` field value starts with one of: `answered-`, `closed-`, `resolved-`. Examples already in repo: `answered-via-chat`, `answered-via-AskUserQuestion`, `answered-2026-05-04-via-chat`. Detection is deterministic (head -20 of file + grep `^status:`).\n2. **No human-veto signal**: bundle frontmatter has NO `wait_until:` ISO-8601 timestamp greater than current epoch — if present, agent MUST defer mv until that timestamp passes. Allows human to override auto-mv per-bundle without contract amendment.\n3. **No global pause**: file `human-workspace/q-and-a/.auto-mv-paused` does NOT exist — global kill switch for the auto-mv mechanism (single empty file presence pauses all auto-mv).\n4. **Hook validation**: the mv is performed by `scripts/hooks/qa-pending-auto-mover.sh` (Stop hook). Direct manual `mv` invocation by agent (e.g. via Bash tool) is STILL forbidden — only the validated hook path is authorized.\n\nAgent never writes to `human-workspace/` outside the designated `q-and-a/pending/` write channel + the auto-mv rule above + `notifications/` write channel (existing).\n","contentDiffersFromDisk":false},"displayPath":"agent-workspace\\CLAUDE.md"},"type":"attachment","uuid":"d748b840-9741-4085-9473-2ffc35cebbc6","timestamp":"2026-05-09T04:38:38.683Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"6de7193e-f2d3-4b9f-a967-6dbdad109e56","version":"2.1.126","gitBranch":"main"}

B/C/D land as D2/D3/D4 secondary signals in same script.\n42\t- **smoke-test-result**: 24 D1 violations detected on first dry-run (pre-existing skills + commands bloat — write-a-skill 163, postgres-pgvector 276, devils-advocate command 250, drift-check 209, master-plan 201, etc.). To be addressed in Track 6 (skill progressive-disclosure refactor) per borrow-list A-12.\n43\t\n44\t### Q-B2 — Hard Block Default for Charter/SCOPE-tier?\n45\t\n46\t- **fire_when**: S5 Track 7 `decision-discipline.md` authoring → fired S15 PLAN 2026-04-29\n47\t- **status**: closed\n48\t- **fired_at**: 2026-04-29 (S15 PLAN Batch 1)\n49\t- **closed_at**: 2026-04-29 (S15 PLAN Batch 1, same turn)\n50\t- **source**: bundle 002 cluster B\n51\t- **question**: Hard rule blocking default-acceptance for charter/SCOPE-tier?\n52\t- **options**: A: Yes, charter/SCOPE-tier MUST require explicit letter pick (already partial in Charter-Tier Split Rule) / B: Yes AND extend to DECISION_ROUTING-tier / C: No, defaults are fine if questions well-designed / D: open answer\n53\t- **answer**: A (Recommended) — charter/SCOPE-tier MUST require explicit letter pick. Default-acceptance only allowed for IMPL-tier or below. To be codified in `proposals/decision-discipline.md` § Tier-vs-default-acceptance during S16 IMPL.\n54\t- **why-needed-in-S5**: codifies in `decision-discipline.md` whether the hard-block extends beyond charter\n55\t\n56\t### Q-C2 — Auto-Context-Loading Mechanism\n57\t\n58\t- **fire_when**: S5 Track 7 `autonomous-protocol.md` authoring OR Phase 1 if signal emerges → fired S15 PLAN 2026-04-29\n59\t- **status**: closed\n60\t- **fired_at**: 2026-04-29 (S15 PLAN Batch 2)\n61\t- **closed_at**: 2026-04-29 (S15 PLAN Batch 2, same turn)\n62\t- **source**: bundle 002 cluster C\n63\t- **question**: Should agents have a \"minimum context\" auto-loader hook, OR keep manual just-in-time loading?\n64\t- **options**: A: UserPromptSubmit hook auto-greps + injects top-5 relevant files (cheap, deterministic) / B: LLM-based context-selector subagent / C: Keep manual just-in-time / D: Hybrid (auto for routine SessionStart; LLM-selector for complex) / E: open answer\n65\t- **answer**: D (Recommended in S15 reframe) — Hybrid: auto for routine SessionStart; LLM-selector for complex. Implements L-S11-1 (Phase 0 hook portability), L-S13-1 (producer-consumer log\n# path mismatch), and S16 success criterion #10 (drift signal D11 / D-IDENTITY extension\n# scanning for fabricated SUPERVISED-mode regression in autonomous_mode=true ONLY-mode\n# stockforge).\n#\n# Wires: Stop hook in .claude/settings.json. Soft-warn only (exit 0 always; never blocks).\n# Output: per-violation lines to .session-hooks.log + (if violations >0) one notification\n# file in human-workspace/notifications/.\n#\n# Per Q-E2 (closed S15 Batch 1): promotion frequency = phase-boundary; this hook detects\n# drift continuously but does NOT auto-promote. Surface to agent who triages next turn.\n# Per Q-E3 (closed S15 Batch 1): promotion priority = hook FIRST, skill SECOND, charter LAST.\n# Phase 0 portability: bash + POSIX only. L-S10-1: if/then/fi only; split-local.\nset -uo pipefail\ntrap 'exit 0' ERR\n\nPROJECT_DIR=\"${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}\"\nHOOKS_DIR=\"$PROJECT_DIR/scripts/hooks\"\nLOG=\"$PROJECT_DIR/agent-workspace/memory/.session-hooks.log\"\nNOTIF_DIR=\"$PROJECT_DIR/human-workspace/notifications\"\nTS=\"$(date -Iseconds 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)\"\n\nmkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$LOG\")\" 2>/dev/null || true\n\nVIOLATIONS=0\nVIOLATION_LIST=\"\"\n\nemit() {\n  local code=\"$1\" file=\"$2\" detail=\"$3\"\n  VIOLATIONS=$(( VIOLATIONS + 1 ))\n  VIOLATION_LIST=\"${VIOLATION_LIST}  - ${code}: ${file} — ${detail}\"$'\\n'\n}\n\n# === Check 1: L-S11-1 Phase 0 portability — no python/jq/yq/pip/npm in scripts/hooks ===\n# Exempt: comments, strings, and explicit Phase-1+ stubs marked with \"# PHASE-1+\" guard comment.\nif [ -d \"$HOOKS_DIR\" ]; then\n  for f in \"$HOOKS_DIR\"/*.sh; do\n    [ ! -f \"$f\" ] && continue\n    bn=\"$(basename \"$f\")\"\n    # Skip self.\n    [ \"$bn\" = \"bash-hook-lint.sh\" ] && continue\n    # Strip comments + heredocs roughly: scan only \"executable\" lines (not # comments).\n    NON_COMMENT=\"$(grep -vE '^[[:space:]]*#' \"$f\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n    # Look for forbidden tooling invocations as command tokens.\n    if printf '%s' \"$NON_COMMENT\" | grep -qE '(^|[[:space:]`(|&;])(python3?|jq|yq|pip|pip3|npm|pnpm)([[:space:]]|$)'; then\n      emit \"L-S11-1-PORTABILITY\" \"$bn\" \"non-Phase-0-portable invocation (python/jq/yq/pip/npm) — Phase 0 doctrine = bash + POSIX only\"\n    fi","numLines":50,"startLine":1,"totalLines":489}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e51be523-e5f0-4b45-a75b-b52895843f68","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"6de7193e-f2d3-4b9f-a967-6dbdad109e56","version":"2.1.126","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"7cb2498a-d8dc-45dd-836e-92a8e9d7bb32","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"nested_memory","path":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\human-workspace\\CLAUDE.md","content":{"path":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\human-workspace\\CLAUDE.md","type":"Project","content":"# human-workspace/ — Contract\n\n> **Audience**: agents reading anything inside `human-workspace/`.\n> **Established**: Decision 002 § Track 1 (Workspace Dualism Foundation).\n\n## Identity\n\n`human-workspace/` is the **human-authored authority** layer. Everything here represents **human decisions, scope, intent, and verification**. Agents READ this directory as authoritative; agents do NOT WRITE here except in one designated channel (`q-and-a/pending/` — where agents post questions for human to answer).\n\nThis separation exists because of a failure mode observed in the orch sister project (orch `decisions/040`, post-mortem CF-DOGFOOD-2): when human and agent share a single workspace without contract, agent silently overrides human-CRITICAL items via technical-defer logic. The user explicitly raised this concern in `human-workspace/user_prompt/20260429_02_init.txt` §1.1.\n\n---\n\n## Subdirectories\n\n| Path | Purpose | Who writes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `user_prompt/` | Human's mid-flight scope/idea drops. Naming: `YYYYMMDD_NN_<slug>.txt`. Files are immutable post-creation (audit trail). | **Human only**. Agent: read-only. |\n| `decisions/` | Human's strategic decisions (e.g., scope-lock, charter-revision-trigger, peer-share authorization). Format: `D-H-<NNN>-<slug>.md`. | **Human only**. Agent: read-only. |\n| `q-and-a/pending/` | **The ONE channel where agent writes inside human-workspace**. Agent drops Q&A bundles per Grill Maximization doctrine; human responds inline or moves to `answered/`. | **Both** (agent writes new bundles; human writes answers). |\n| `q-and-a/answered/` | Q&A bundles human has answered (with answers inline). Agent reads answers and acts. Archive forever; provenance trail. | **Human moves** (drag from `pending/`); agent reads. |\n| `q-and-a/stale/` | Pending bundles that exceeded their `expected_answer_by` (default 24h, urgent 4h). Auto-moved by SessionStart hook (Track 5). | **Hook moves**; both read. |\n| `notifications/` | Agent push to human — summaries, alerts, milestones. Format: `N-<TS>-<level>-<slug>.md`. Levels: INFO / SUMMARY / ALERT. | **Agent writes**; human reads. |\n\n---\n\n## Contract Rules (BINDING)\n\n1. **Agent NEVER writes to `user_prompt/` or `decisions/`.** Permission deny. If something feels like a human decision but the human hasn't written it, dispatch a Q&A bundle into `q-and-a/pending/` instead.\n\n2. **`user_prompt/` files are immutable post-creation.** Agent never deletes, renames, or edits these files. They are the audit trail of human interventions.\n\n3. **Q&A bundle format** (per Track 4 spec): YAML frontmatter (`id`, `topic`, `opened_at`, `expected_answer_by`, `priority`, `related_decisions`, `status`) + question body + answer-fill section. Each bundle = up to 25 questions per Grill Maximization doctrine; split if more.\n\n4. **Agent does not move files between `q-and-a/{pending,answered,stale}/`** — that's human's role (or hook's, in case of stale). Agent only WRITES new pending bundles and READS answers.\n\n5. **Notifications are read-only for human.** Human shouldn't edit `notifications/*` files; if disagreement with notification content, drop a `user_prompt/` instead. Notifications are the agent's account of events.\n\n6. **Provenance pointers**: every `agent-workspace/memory/decisions/D-<NNN>` should reference its source `human-workspace/user_prompt/<file>` or `human-workspace/decisions/D-H-<NNN>` if the agent's decision derives from human input.\n\n7. **No multi-tenancy in this directory.** Per identity-scope.md (Track 7), stockforge is single-tenant Phase 0-5; if peer share happens it's via git-fork, each peer has their own `human-workspace/` per fork.\n\n---\n\n## Reading Priority for Agent\n\nWhen loading session context (per `agent-workspace/constitution/autonomous-protocol.md` Track 7):\n\n1. **All `user_prompt/*.txt`** — every prompt, not just most recent. Per orch lesson (decision-040 USER-CRITICAL tier): never silently defer human-CRITICAL items.\n2. **All `decisions/*.md`** — human's strategic decisions are above charter principles in priority; charter is below human (charter was authored by human in the first place).\n3. **Latest `q-and-a/answered/*`** — recent answers update agent's understanding.\n4. **Open `q-and-a/pending/*`** — flag if waiting on human answers.\n5. **Recent `notifications/*`** (last 7 days) — for situational awareness.\n\n---\n\n## Agent Self-Check\n\nBefore writing to `human-workspace/q-and-a/pending/`, ask:\n- Is this question genuinely something the human must decide? (Charter principle / Charter-affecting / SCOPE-affecting / above ARCH-tier confidence threshold)\n- Have I already grilled this in a recent bundle? (Don't ask twice; don't pollute pending/)\n- Am I bundling per Grill Maximization (target 15-20 questions, max 25)? Or splintering across many small bundles?\n\nIf the answer to any is \"I'm not sure\" — DON'T write the bundle. Either answer it yourself with charter-principle reasoning + log decision, or wait until ambiguity clears.\n","contentDiffersFromDisk":false},"displayPath":"human-workspace\\CLAUDE.md"},"type":"attachment","uuid":"73b6c532-e1d5-4063-a96f-d629f6c82361","timestamp":"2026-05-09T04:39:10.534Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"6de7193e-f2d3-4b9f-a967-6dbdad109e56","version":"2.1.126","gitBranch":"main"}

Empirical proof:\n  #   $ printf \"foo \\\\\\\\\\nbar\\n\" > /tmp/t && awk \"/\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\$/\" /tmp/t  → matches line 1.\n  # WRONG regex (pre-S204): /\\\\$/ — 2 backslashes in awk-source = 1 backslash in regex,\n  # which escapes the `$` to mean literal `$` CHARACTER (not EOL anchor). That regex\n  # matched any line containing `$` anywhere, breaking continuation logic. Pre-existing\n  # bug since Check 7 inception (S58); discovered S203, fixed S204 (L-S203-1).\n  if (line ~ /\\\\\\\\$/) {\n    sub(/\\\\\\\\$/, \"\", line)\n    carry = (carry == \"\" ? line : carry \" \" line)\n    next\n  }\n  full = (carry == \"\" ? line : carry \" \" line)\n  carry = \"\"\n\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*set[[:space:]]+\\+o[[:space:]]+pipefail/) {\n    pipefail_off = 1\n    next\n  }\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*set[[:space:]]+-[a-zA-Z]*o[[:space:]]+pipefail/) {\n    pipefail_off = 0\n    next\n  }\n  if (pipefail_off) next\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*#/) next\n  if (full ~ /grep[[:space:]]/) {\n    if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*(if|while|until|elif)[[:space:]]/) next\n    if (full ~ /grep[[:space:]].*[[:space:]](&&|\\|\\|)[[:space:]]/) next\n    print NR \":\" full\n    exit\n  }\n}\n' \"$f\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n      if [ -n \"$VIOLATION_LINE\" ]; then\n        emit \"L-S48d-1-PIPEFAIL-BARE-GREP\" \"$bn\" \"pipefail + ERR trap + grep without guard — silent fail risk (L-S48d-1; refined S58 to recognize compound-if + alt-guard echo/exit/return + pipefail-bracket + multi-line continuation)\"","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"67375e6a-755c-467c-b2c0-7fbb9eac1443","timestamp":"2026-05-09T04:46:17.937Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"    if [ \"$HAS_PIPEFAIL\" = 1 ] && [ \"$HAS_ERR_TRAP\" = 1 ]; then\n      VIOLATION_LINE=\"$(awk '\nBEGIN { pipefail_off = 1; carry = \"\" }\n{\n  line = $0\n  # Match physical line ending with literal `\\` (backslash continuation).\n  # CORRECT regex: /\\\\\\\\$/ — 4 backslashes in awk-source literal collapse to 2 in the\n  # compiled regex, which means literal `\\` + `$` EOL anchor. That regex\n  # matched any line containing `$` anywhere, breaking continuation logic. Pre-existing\n  # bug since Check 7 inception (S58); discovered S203, fixed S204 (L-S203-1).\n  if (line ~ /\\\\\\\\$/) {\n    sub(/\\\\\\\\$/, \"\", line)\n    carry = (carry == \"\" ? line : carry \" \" line)\n    next\n  }\n  full = (carry == \"\" ? line : carry \" \" line)\n  carry = \"\"\n\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*set[[:space:]]+\\+o[[:space:]]+pipefail/) {\n    pipefail_off = 1\n    next\n  }\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*set[[:space:]]+-[a-zA-Z]*o[[:space:]]+pipefail/) {\n    pipefail_off = 0\n    next\n  }\n  if (pipefail_off) next\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*#/) next\n  if (full ~ /grep[[:space:]]/) {\n    if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*(if|while|until|elif)[[:space:]]/) next\n    if (full ~ /grep[[:space:]].*[[:space:]](&&|\\|\\|)[[:space:]]/) next\n    print NR \":\" full\n    exit\n  }","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8110df6a-61aa-4883-b239-c62d0fdcb762","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"6de7193e-f2d3-4b9f-a967-6dbdad109e56","version":"2.1.126","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"535f7f9a-5724-4ca4-9dcd-c7dc6587755a","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01CfoSN88GLeL7amg9RLK9oK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01XPxJH1Fhkmq89FLGQ7fhf5","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd C:/htdocs/stockforge && \\\necho '=== Test with VERBATIM production awk source as a heredoc to file ===' && \\\ncat > /tmp/check7-awk.txt <<'AWKHEREDOC'\n\nBEGIN { pipefail_off = 1; carry = \"\" }\n{\n  line = $0\n  # Match physical line ending with literal `\\` (backslash continuation).\n  # CORRECT regex: /\\\\\\\\$/ — 4 backslashes in awk-source literal collapse to 2 in the\n  # compiled regex, which means literal `\\` + `$` EOL anchor. Empirical proof:\n  #   $ printf \"foo \\\\\\\\\\nbar\\n\" > /tmp/t && awk \"/\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\$/\" /tmp/t  → matches line 1.\n  # WRONG regex (pre-S204): /\\\\$/ — 2 backslashes in awk-source = 1 backslash in regex,\n  # which escapes the `$` to mean literal `$` CHARACTER (not EOL anchor). That regex\n  # matched any line containing `$` anywhere, breaking continuation logic. Pre-existing\n  # bug since Check 7 inception (S58); discovered S203, fixed S204 (L-S203-1).\n  if (line ~ /\\\\\\\\$/) {\n    sub(/\\\\\\\\$/, \"\", line)\n    carry = (carry == \"\" ? line : carry \" \" line)\n    next\n  }\n  full = (carry == \"\" ? line : carry \" \" line)\n  carry = \"\"\n\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*set[[:space:]]+\\+o[[:space:]]+pipefail/) {\n    pipefail_off = 1\n    next\n  }\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*set[[:space:]]+-[a-zA-Z]*o[[:space:]]+pipefail/) {\n    pipefail_off = 0\n    next\n  }\n  if (pipefail_off) next\n  if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*#/) next\n  if (full ~ /grep[[:space:]]/) {\n    if (full ~ /^[[:space:]]*(if|while|until|elif)[[:space:]]/) next\n    if (full ~ /grep[[:space:]].*[[:space:]](&&|\\|\\|)[[:space:]]/) next\n    print NR \":\" full\n    exit\n  }\n}\nAWKHEREDOC\nawk -f /tmp/check7-awk.txt scripts/hooks/drift-rollup-daily.sh","description":"Run with EXACT awk source from heredoc"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1314,"cache_read_input_tokens":150420,"output_tokens":2806,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1314,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[{"input_tokens":1,"output_tokens":2806,"cache_read_input_tokens":150420,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1314,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1314},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"}},"requestId":"req_011CarPs4CeN6yAuDB2jv3sB","type":"assistant","uuid":"c35a389f-4f42-4c1f-9baf-7895dd311f54","timestamp":"2026-05-09T04:51:53.355Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"6de7193e-f2d3-4b9f-a967-6dbdad109e56","version":"2.1.126","gitBranch":"main"}

Each rule earned through real experience (post-mortem, drift detection, user correction).\n4\t> Rules here have higher status than convention but lower than constitution.\n5\t\n6\t## Rule Format\n7\t\n8\tUse this template:\n9\t\n10\t```markdown\n11\t### YYYY-MM-DD: [Short rule name]\n12\t**Context**: What situation triggered learning this\n13\t**Rule**: The actionable rule\n14\t**Anti-example**: What was done wrong\n15\t**Correct example**: What should be done instead\n16\t**Severity**: critical | high | medium | low\n17\t**Auto-detect**: yes/no — can a drift signal catch this?\n18\t```\n19\t\n20\t---\n21\t\n22\t## Recent Rules (digest; last 5)\n23\t\n24\t### 2026-05-09 (S203): Guardian Check 7 awk regex `/\\\\$/` is double-broken — L-S203-1\n25\t**Context**: S203 attempted cheapest inline-comment strip to bash-hook-lint Check 7 awk → broke 4 prior-passing fixtures. Diagnostic awk debug revealed `/\\\\$/` regex (intended to match literal `\\` at EOL for line-continuation join) actually matches any line containing `$` character. Cause: awk parses `\\\\$` as `\\$` (escaped EOL anchor) = literal `$`. Pre-existing bug since Check 7 inception — broke multi-line `$(...)` join across all hooks Check 7 ever scanned. Direct application of L-S201-1 (Guardian itself bug); revert + escalate to dedicated session.\n26\t**Rule**: When Guardian regex behavior is unexpected, write a 3-line awk diagnostic before assuming source code is wrong. Multi-line continuation in awk requires `/\\\\\\\\$/` (4-backslash literal = 2 in regex = 1 `\\` char + EOL anchor); `/\\\\$/` does NOT work as continuation match.\n27\t**Anti-example**: Add inline-comment strip on top of broken multi-line continuation logic without verifying continuation regex first.\n28\t**Correct example**: S203 wrote `echo 'X=$(foo)' | awk '$0 ~ /\\\\$/' && echo MATCH` diagnostic → confirmed unexpected MATCH → reverted cleanly (39/39 PASS restored) + queued L-S203-1 for dedicated awk-refactor session (S204 PRIORITY 2).\n29\t**Severity**: high\n30\t**Auto-detect**: maybe — awk-regex sanity-check pre-flight when Check 7 awk-script is edited. Lower priority since bug is identified.\n31\t**Promotion target**: dedicated awk-refactor session — fix `/\\\\\\\\$/` + add 3 firing-test cases (TC-C-multiline-cmdsub + TC-C-inline-comment + TC-C-realistic-multiline). ~50 LOC across 2 files. MEDIUM-HIGH risk (Check 7 touches 50+ hooks).\n32\t\n33\t### 2026-05-09 (S201): Guardians need calibration too — verify Guardian claim against ground-truth code BEFORE acting — L-S201-1\n34\t**Context**: S201 entered with S200 checkpoint plan to fix \"3 hook bug classes\" identified via grep pass + bash-hook-lint output. L-S200-1 (just-minted) said \"inspect Guardian BEFORE LLM hypothesis cycle\". S201 applied L-S200-1 to S200's own output — verbatim re-read of 5 affected scripts revealed: (a) effort-escalation guard at line 33-34 actually accepts both UserPromptSubmit AND PreToolUse+Agent (S200 misread as MIS-WIRED); (b) idle-escape + phase-coherence + adr-staleness ALREADY had L-S108-1 date-hour-bucket fix — bash-hook-lint Check 6b regex `\\$\\{?[A-Z_]` matched `${BUCKET}` (the FIX) as if it were the BUG → 3 false-positive flags emitted at every Stop since S195, distracted 13-cycle hypothesis hunt; (c) only Bug Class 3 (HH-1 empty-SID) was a real bug.\n35\t**Rule**: When acting on Guardian (lint, drift-signal, hook-firing analysis) output, MUST verbatim-read the source code Guardian flagged before classifying as bug + before applying fix. Guardian regex/heuristic itself can have false positives; \"Guardian-first\" doctrine pairs with \"ground-truth-first\" verification.\n36\t**Anti-example**: Trust S200 checkpoint's \"3 bug classes\" framing + dispatch fixes per checkpoint plan without verifying each framing against the actual script. S200 itself trusted bash-hook-lint output verbatim across 13 hypothesis cycles without verifying the lint regex was correct.\n37\t**Correct example**: S201 read all 5 scripts verbatim (5 parallel Read calls) BEFORE any Edit. Caught 2 of 3 framings as misdiagnosis. Found Guardian-itself bug. Fixed real bug + Guardian regex bug in single session (~21 LOC across 2 files).\n38\t**Severity**: high\n39\t**Auto-detect**: yes — bash-hook-lint Check-N addition: detect when an Edit/Write tool call references a hook flagged in latest lint output WITHOUT a paired Read tool call on that hook in same turn.\n40\t**Promotion target**: hook (Check-N in bash-hook-lint.sh) + skill (verify-Guardian-claim subroutine in vbw-protocol).\n41\t\n42\t### 2026-05-09 (S201): Verify detection signal against producing script — L-S201-2\n43\t**Context**: S187..S200 spent 13 hypothesis cycles on a \"`.effort-escalation.log` MISSING = SILENT\" detection signal. S201 verbatim-read effort-escalation-detector.sh confirmed the script never writes to that log file (only stderr at line 108). The log MISSING was always EXPECTED, not a SILENT signal. Wrong detection signal misled entire investigation.\n44\t**Rule**: When using file-existence detection (e.g., `ls .<hook>.log` MISSING → SILENT), MUST grep the producing hook script for the matching write site BEFORE classifying. If hook script has no matching write site (no `>> \"$LOGFILE\"`, `tee \"$LOGFILE\"`, `echo ... > \"$LOGFILE\"`), then \"log MISSING\" is invalid as a SILENT signal — replace with stderr capture or process-trace based detection.\n45\t**Anti-example**: Run hypothesis tests on hook chain truncation using `.effort-escalation.log MISSING` as evidence of hook silent-exit, when the hook by design only emits to stderr. 13 cycles of phantom-chasing.\n46\t**Correct example**: For each hook in detection signal table, grep the hook script for write sites + use those write sites as ground-truth detection signal. If hook only emits to stderr → capture stderr stream (not log file existence) for SILENT verification.\n47\t**Severity**: high\n48\t**Auto-detect**: yes — pre-flight checker that, given a hook detection signal table, validates each `<file>` MISSING/PRESENT signal against grep of the producing hook script.\n49\t**Promotion target**: hook (detection-signal-validator.sh pre-flight) OR skill (verify-detection-signal subroutine).\n50\t\n51\t### 2026-05-09 (S200): Inspect deterministic Guardian output BEFORE launching ≥2nd hypothesis cycle on same symptom — L-S200-1\n52\t**Context**: S187..S199 ran 13 hypothesis cycles on chain-truncation framing for hooks #5/#6/#7/#8/#9 silence. bash-hook-lint hook output at every Stop since S195 explicitly named L-S108-1 root cause + fix suggestion. Agent missed lint output across all 13 cycles → S200 falsified entire chain-truncation framing only by grep pass on the 4 silent hook scripts directly.\n53\t**Rule**: When deterministic Guardian (bash-hook-lint, drift-signals, Stop-hook diagnostic output) emits hypothesized root cause for a recurring symptom, agent MUST inspect Guardian output + lint-flagged files before launching ≥2nd hypothesis cycle on same symptom. Don't drown the Guardian signal in the LLM exploration noise.\n54\t**Anti-example**: Run 13 LLM-driven hypothesis tests (H-c / H-a / H-e / H-d.1 / H-d.2 / H-f / H-g.1) without inspecting bash-hook-lint output that named specific hooks + specific root cause + specific fix.\n55\t**Correct example**: At S200, before launching H-g.2 (next chain-truncation hypothesis), read bash-hook-lint output → grep the 4 silent hook scripts for the lint-flagged pattern → identify 3 distinct hook bug classes that explain symptom. Symptom: ≥3 hypothesis tests across consecutive sessions without referencing latest Guardian output.\n63\t**Anti-example**: S187..S199 hypothesis sequence (H-c → H-a → H-e → H-d.1 → H-d.2 → H-f → H-g.1) without any reference to bash-hook-lint output flag-list.\n64\t**Correct example**: After 1st failed hypothesis test, re-inspect ALL Guardian outputs (bash-hook-lint + drift-signals + Stop-hook diagnostic) for any flag related to same script(s). Each rule earned through real experience (post-mortem, drift detection, user correction).\n> Rules here have higher status than convention but lower than constitution.\n\n## Rule Format\n\nUse this template:\n\n```markdown\n### YYYY-MM-DD: [Short rule name]\n**Context**: What situation triggered learning this\n**Rule**: The actionable rule\n**Anti-example**: What was done wrong\n**Correct example**: What should be done instead\n**Severity**: critical | high | medium | low\n**Auto-detect**: yes/no — can a drift signal catch this?\n```\n\n---\n\n## Recent Rules (digest; last 5)\n\n### 2026-05-09 (S203): Guardian Check 7 awk regex `/\\\\$/` is double-broken — L-S203-1\n**Context**: S203 attempted cheapest inline-comment strip to bash-hook-lint Check 7 awk → broke 4 prior-passing fixtures. Diagnostic awk debug revealed `/\\\\$/` regex (intended to match literal `\\` at EOL for line-continuation join) actually matches any line containing `$` character. Cause: awk parses `\\\\$` as `\\$` (escaped EOL anchor) = literal `$`. Pre-existing bug since Check 7 inception — broke multi-line `$(...)` join across all hooks Check 7 ever scanned. Direct application of L-S201-1 (Guardian itself bug); revert + escalate to dedicated session.\n**Rule**: When Guardian regex behavior is unexpected, write a 3-line awk diagnostic before assuming source code is wrong. Multi-line continuation in awk requires `/\\\\\\\\$/` (4-backslash literal = 2 in regex = 1 `\\` char + EOL anchor); `/\\\\$/` does NOT work as continuation match.\n**Anti-example**: Add inline-comment strip on top of broken multi-line continuation logic without verifying continuation regex first.\n**Correct example**: S203 wrote `echo 'X=$(foo)' | awk '$0 ~ /\\\\$/' && echo MATCH` diagnostic → confirmed unexpected MATCH → reverted cleanly (39/39 PASS restored) + queued L-S203-1 for dedicated awk-refactor session (S204 PRIORITY 2).\n**Severity**: high\n**Auto-detect**: maybe — awk-regex sanity-check pre-flight when Check 7 awk-script is edited. Lower priority since bug is identified.\n**Promotion target**: dedicated awk-refactor session — fix `/\\\\\\\\$/` + add 3 firing-test cases (TC-C-multiline-cmdsub + TC-C-inline-comment + TC-C-realistic-multiline). ~50 LOC across 2 files. MEDIUM-HIGH risk (Check 7 touches 50+ hooks).\n\n### 2026-05-09 (S201): Guardians need calibration too — verify Guardian claim against ground-truth code BEFORE acting — L-S201-1\n**Context**: S201 entered with S200 checkpoint plan to fix \"3 hook bug classes\" identified via grep pass + bash-hook-lint output. L-S200-1 (just-minted) said \"inspect Guardian BEFORE LLM hypothesis cycle\". S201 applied L-S200-1 to S200's own output — verbatim re-read of 5 affected scripts revealed: (a) effort-escalation guard at line 33-34 actually accepts both UserPromptSubmit AND PreToolUse+Agent (S200 misread as MIS-WIRED); (b) idle-escape + phase-coherence + adr-staleness ALREADY had L-S108-1 date-hour-bucket fix — bash-hook-lint Check 6b regex `\\$\\{?[A-Z_]` matched `${BUCKET}` (the FIX) as if it were the BUG → 3 false-positive flags emitted at every Stop since S195, distracted 13-cycle hypothesis hunt; (c) only Bug Class 3 (HH-1 empty-SID) was a real bug.\n**Rule**: When acting on Guardian (lint, drift-signal, hook-firing analysis) output, MUST verbatim-read the source code Guardian flagged before classifying as bug + before applying fix. Guardian regex/heuristic itself can have false positives; \"Guardian-first\" doctrine pairs with \"ground-truth-first\" verification.\n**Anti-example**: Trust S200 checkpoint's \"3 bug classes\" framing + dispatch fixes per checkpoint plan without verifying each framing against the actual script. S200 itself trusted bash-hook-lint output verbatim across 13 hypothesis cycles without verifying the lint regex was correct.\n**Correct example**: S201 read all 5 scripts verbatim (5 parallel Read calls) BEFORE any Edit. Caught 2 of 3 framings as misdiagnosis. Found Guardian-itself bug. Fixed real bug + Guardian regex bug in single session (~21 LOC across 2 files).\n**Severity**: high\n**Auto-detect**: yes — bash-hook-lint Check-N addition: detect when an Edit/Write tool call references a hook flagged in latest lint output WITHOUT a paired Read tool call on that hook in same turn.\n**Promotion target**: hook (Check-N in bash-hook-lint.sh) + skill (verify-Guardian-claim subroutine in vbw-protocol).\n\n### 2026-05-09 (S201): Verify detection signal against producing script — L-S201-2\n**Context**: S187..S200 spent 13 hypothesis cycles on a \"`.effort-escalation.log` MISSING = SILENT\" detection signal. S201 verbatim-read effort-escalation-detector.sh confirmed the script never writes to that log file (only stderr at line 108). The log MISSING was always EXPECTED, not a SILENT signal. Wrong detection signal misled entire investigation.\n**Rule**: When using file-existence detection (e.g., `ls .<hook>.log` MISSING → SILENT), MUST grep the producing hook script for the matching write site BEFORE classifying. If hook script has no matching write site (no `>> \"$LOGFILE\"`, `tee \"$LOGFILE\"`, `echo ... > \"$LOGFILE\"`), then \"log MISSING\" is invalid as a SILENT signal — replace with stderr capture or process-trace based detection.\n**Anti-example**: Run hypothesis tests on hook chain truncation using `.effort-escalation.log MISSING` as evidence of hook silent-exit, when the hook by design only emits to stderr. 13 cycles of phantom-chasing.\n**Correct example**: For each hook in detection signal table, grep the hook script for write sites + use those write sites as ground-truth detection signal. If hook only emits to stderr → capture stderr stream (not log file existence) for SILENT verification.\n**Severity**: high\n**Auto-detect**: yes — pre-flight checker that, given a hook detection signal table, validates each `<file>` MISSING/PRESENT signal against grep of the producing hook script.\n**Promotion target**: hook (detection-signal-validator.sh pre-flight) OR skill (verify-detection-signal subroutine).\n\n### 2026-05-09 (S200): Inspect deterministic Guardian output BEFORE launching ≥2nd hypothesis cycle on same symptom — L-S200-1\n**Context**: S187..S199 ran 13 hypothesis cycles on chain-truncation framing for hooks #5/#6/#7/#8/#9 silence. bash-hook-lint hook output at every Stop since S195 explicitly named L-S108-1 root cause + fix suggestion. Agent missed lint output across all 13 cycles → S200 falsified entire chain-truncation framing only by grep pass on the 4 silent hook scripts directly.\n**Rule**: When deterministic Guardian (bash-hook-lint, drift-signals, Stop-hook diagnostic output) emits hypothesized root cause for a recurring symptom, agent MUST inspect Guardian output + lint-flagged files before launching ≥2nd hypothesis cycle on same symptom. Don't drown the Guardian signal in the LLM exploration noise.\n**Anti-example**: Run 13 LLM-driven hypothesis tests (H-c / H-a / H-e / H-d.1 / H-d.2 / H-f / H-g.1) without inspecting bash-hook-lint output that named specific hooks + specific root cause + specific fix.\n**Correct example**: At S200, before launching H-g.2 (next chain-truncation hypothesis), read bash-hook-lint output → grep the 4 silent hook scripts for the lint-flagged pattern → identify 3 distinct hook bug classes that explain symptom. (Note: at S201, applying L-S200-1 RECURSIVELY caught that 2 of those 3 framings were also wrong because the lint regex itself had a false positive — see L-S201-1.)\n**Severity**: critical\n**Auto-detect**: yes — pre-flight check that, before launching a hypothesis test (Edit/Write to hook code labeled `H-<x>`), grep the latest bash-hook-lint output + Stop-hook diagnostic output for any flag related to the same script(s) — block launch if Guardian output has flags not yet inspected.\n**Promotion target**: hook (Check-N in bash-hook-lint.sh) + skill (Guardian-output-inspect-first subroutine) + charter (if recurrence persists).\n\n### 2026-05-09 (S200): LLM-driven hypothesis exploration over-ran missed lint signal — AP-24\n**Context**: Same as L-S200-1. 13-cycle phantom-chase across 10-day span; ~50K main tokens spent on hypothesis cycles when 5-min Guardian output read would have ended investigation.\n**Rule**: AP-24 anti-pattern: LLM-driven hypothesis exploration over-runs when explicit deterministic Guardian root-cause annotation present in working memory output. Symptom: ≥3 hypothesis tests across consecutive sessions without referencing latest Guardian output.\n**Anti-example**: S187..S199 hypothesis sequence (H-c → H-a → H-e → H-d.1 → H-d.2 → H-f → H-g.1) without any reference to bash-hook-lint output flag-list.\n**Correct example**: After 1st failed hypothesis test, re-inspect ALL Guardian outputs (bash-hook-lint + drift-signals + Stop-hook diagnostic) for any flag related to same script(s). Treat Guardian output as outranking LLM intuition. (S201 demonstrated proper application.)\n**Severity**: high\n**Auto-detect**: yes — same hook as L-S200-1.\n**Promotion target**: paired with L-S200-1.\n\n\n\n### 2026-05-07 (S176): File-pattern hooks MUST validate against real-state inventory + ship companion firing-test — L-S176-1\n**Context**: HH-C.2 audit (D-038) found project-md-staleness-check.sh was double-broken no-op since 2026-05-06 inception. Zero WARN entries in 10836-line .session-hooks.log = silent failure for 22+ sessions. Per Phase 3.5 Hard Rule #2 (every hook ships with companion firing-test).\n**Anti-example**: Hook regex authored from assumed file format (e.g., `**Phase**: N` key-value style) without grepping target file. Hook glob authored from assumed naming convention (e.g., `D-*.md`) without listing directory. Ships passing manual eyeball review but silently no-ops in production.\n**Correct example**: Pre-flight grep + ls + companion firing-test exhibit both bugs as deliberate fixtures (e.g., assert RED-state WARN when stale fixture > tolerance threshold).\n**Why**: Charter Principle 8 (Calibration over confidence) demands deterministic over LLM-Guardian. Verify-Before-Write Protocol applies to hook authoring same as code.\n**How to apply**: Pre-S177-IMPL audit candidate list — scan all `scripts/hooks/*.sh` for file-pattern parsers without companion firing-test; promote-rule entry. Empirical re-verification using `Write` tool (preserves bytes exactly) + `awk -f file.awk` + `od -c` proved the OPPOSITE: original `/\\\\$/` (2 backslashes literal in awk source → regex `\\\\` = literal `\\` + EOL `$` anchor) IS CORRECT and DOES match `\\` at EOL. S203 diagnostic was misled by Bash-tool I/O layer that double-strips backslashes through inline single-quoted commands. L-S203-1 framing is FALSE; reverted + retired with prejudice.\n**Rule**: When a checkpoint/lesson claims a regex (or any escape-laden code) behaves wrongly, verify EMPIRICALLY before acting: (1) write minimal awk via `Write` tool to a file (preserves exact bytes); (2) run via `awk -f file.awk`; (3) verify byte content via `od -c`. NEVER trust bash inline `awk '...'` for diagnostic purposes — bash + tool-shim quoting strips backslashes. L-S201-1 (Guardian-needs-calibration) applies recursively to ALL memory artifacts including checkpoints + lessons.\n**Anti-example**: S203 ran `echo 'X=$(foo)' | awk '$0 ~ /\\\\$/' && echo MATCH` via Bash tool (strips backslash count) → got false MATCH → concluded regex broken → wrote checkpoint claim → S204 acted on claim without re-verification → 4× the violation count emerged.\n**Correct example**: S204 used `Write` tool to author `/tmp/test-2bs.awk` with literal `/\\\\$/` (verified via `od -c` showing 2 backslash bytes) + `awk -f` it on actual file → confirmed correct match → reverted regression cleanly (39/39 firing tests PASS + lint back to 11 violations baseline).\n**Severity**: critical\n**Auto-detect**: maybe — pre-flight check at any `Edit` to a hook script that contains `awk '/\\\\` or similar escape-dense regex: warn agent to use `Write` + `awk -f` + `od -c` triple-verify before commit.\n**Promotion target**: skill (verify-regex-empirically subroutine in vbw-protocol) + charter doctrine entry (cheapest-by-RISK applies to memory-trust too — verify before commit even when memory says \"fix is X\"). Empirical re-verification using `Write` tool (preserves bytes exactly) + `awk -f file.awk` + `od -c` proved the OPPOSITE: original `/\\\\$/` (2 backslashes literal in awk source → regex `\\\\` = literal `\\` + EOL `$` anchor) IS CORRECT and DOES match `\\` at EOL. S203 diagnostic was misled by Bash-tool I/O layer that double-strips backslashes through inline single-quoted commands. L-S203-1 framing is FALSE; reverted + retired with prejudice.\n**Rule**: When a checkpoint/lesson claims a regex (or any escape-laden code) behaves wrongly, verify EMPIRICALLY before acting: (1) write minimal awk via `Write` tool to a file (preserves exact bytes); (2) run via `awk -f file.awk`; (3) verify byte content via `od -c`. NEVER trust bash inline `awk '...'` for diagnostic purposes — bash + tool-shim quoting strips backslashes. L-S201-1 (Guardian-needs-calibration) applies recursively to ALL memory artifacts including checkpoints + lessons.\n**Anti-example**: S203 ran `echo 'X=$(foo)' | awk '$0 ~ /\\\\$/' && echo MATCH` via Bash tool (strips backslash count) → got false MATCH → concluded regex broken → wrote checkpoint claim → S204 acted on claim without re-verification → 4× the violation count emerged.\n**Correct example**: S204 used `Write` tool to author `/tmp/test-2bs.awk` with literal `/\\\\$/` (verified via `od -c` showing 2 backslash bytes) + `awk -f` it on actual file → confirmed correct match → reverted regression cleanly (39/39 firing tests PASS + lint back to 11 violations baseline).\n**Severity**: critical\n**Auto-detect**: maybe — pre-flight check at any `Edit` to a hook script that contains `awk '/\\\\` or similar escape-dense regex: warn agent to use `Write` + `awk -f` + `od -c` triple-verify before commit.\n**Promotion target**: skill (verify-regex-empirically subroutine in vbw-protocol) + charter doctrine entry (cheapest-by-RISK applies to memory-trust too — verify before commit even when memory says \"fix is X\"). NO hook fix needed (production regex is correct as-is).","originalFile":null,"structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":21,"oldLines":14,"newStart":21,"newLines":14,"lines":[" "," ## Recent Rules (digest; last 5)"," ","-### 2026-05-09 (S203): Guardian Check 7 awk regex `/\\\\$/` is double-broken — L-S203-1","-**Context**: S203 attempted cheapest inline-comment strip to bash-hook-lint Check 7 awk → broke 4 prior-passing fixtures. Diagnostic awk debug revealed `/\\\\$/` regex (intended to match literal `\\` at EOL for line-continuation join) actually matches any line containing `$` character. Cause: awk parses `\\\\$` as `\\$` (escaped EOL anchor) = literal `$`. Pre-existing bug since Check 7 inception — broke multi-line `$(...)` join across all hooks Check 7 ever scanned. Direct application of L-S201-1 (Guardian itself bug); revert + escalate to dedicated session.","-**Rule**: When Guardian regex behavior is unexpected, write a 3-line awk diagnostic before assuming source code is wrong.
